# Fila de folhas do Gbzin

Gerado por `python ferramentas/folhas_gbzin.py --md`. Edite lá, não aqui. Ficha em [`docs/fichas/gbzin.md`](../fichas/gbzin.md).

Cada folha vai salva em `sprites/gbzin/quadros/folhas_arcade/<nome>.png`. **Reanexe a referência em toda folha** e espere uma terminar antes de pedir a próxima.

## `conceito`

Anexar: as 6 fotos do Gbzin (branch gbzin-fotos, fora da mugen)

```text
Generate ONE image with your image generation tool. Do not write code and do not edit any file.

The attached photos show the real young man this character is based on. LIKENESS IS THE PRIORITY: anyone who knows him must recognize him at once. Copy from the photos, as faithfully as pixel art allows: the shape of his face (round, full cheeks), his hair (cut, volume, wave, fringe and fade), his eyebrows and eyes, his thin moustache and small goatee, his skin tone and his real body shape and proportions (big, heavy and stocky, as in the full-body photos; do not slim him down and do not turn him into a lean muscular hero). Only the art style (1990s arcade fighting game pixel art) and the outfit below are invented. Character: Gbzin, the jiu-jitsu goat shepherd: a young man with light tanned skin (warm golden-tan, not pink), dark-brown WAVY MESSY hair (loose tousled waves, NOT curly, no ringlets) that is thick and voluminous on top with a heavy messy fringe falling over the forehead to near the eyebrows and a short fade on the sides, big round full face with full cheeks, thick dark eyebrows, heavy-lidded calm brown eyes, a thin sparse moustache and a small sparse goatee on the chin only (clean cheeks, no full beard), serious relaxed expression. Big heavy stocky build: wide soft-strong torso, thick arms, thick legs, heavy like a grappler (strong and bulky, not lean, not a bodybuilder with defined abs). Outfit: black sleeveless tank top, white gym shorts with a thin black side stripe, a brown jiu-jitsu belt tied around the waist over the shorts with the two ends hanging, black sneakers, a gold wristwatch on the left wrist. He carries a SHORT wooden shepherd's crook (about as long as his arm, with a curved hook at the top) in the BACK hand.

Sheet layout, flat solid magenta background (#FF00FF), no text: 1) full body front view, standing, 2) full body side view facing right in a low grappler stance, crook in the back hand, 3) full body back view, 4) a big bust portrait of the face. Same scale for the three full bodies.

Art style: hand-pixeled 1990s arcade fighting game sprite. Heroic anatomy: broad shoulders, thick strong torso, big hands and feet; he is the heavy grappler of the cast, stocky and solid but not fat. Shading in 2-3 large hard-edged flat planes per form, no gradients, no dithering, no noise, no texture. Shadows shift hue: skin shadows toward wine red, highlights toward warm cream; white clothes get cool lilac shadows; black clothes get blue-grey highlights. 1-pixel colored outline (dark wine on skin, dark blue-purple on clothes), never pure black, lighter on the lit side. Small hard 1-3 pixel highlights. Limited palette look.


```

## `referencia_ginga`

Anexar: conceito_v3.png

```text
Generate ONE image with your image generation tool. Do not write code and do not edit any file.

The attached sprite sheet shows the exact character to keep: Gbzin, the jiu-jitsu goat shepherd: a young man with light tanned skin (warm golden-tan, not pink), dark-brown WAVY MESSY hair (loose tousled waves, NOT curly, no ringlets) that is thick and voluminous on top with a heavy messy fringe falling over the forehead to near the eyebrows and a short fade on the sides, big round full face with full cheeks, thick dark eyebrows, heavy-lidded calm brown eyes, a thin sparse moustache and a small sparse goatee on the chin only (clean cheeks, no full beard), serious relaxed expression. Big heavy stocky build: wide soft-strong torso, thick arms, thick legs, heavy like a grappler (strong and bulky, not lean, not a bodybuilder with defined abs). Outfit: black sleeveless tank top, white gym shorts with a thin black side stripe, a brown jiu-jitsu belt tied around the waist over the shorts with the two ends hanging, black sneakers, a gold wristwatch on the left wrist. He carries a SHORT wooden shepherd's crook (about as long as his arm, with a curved hook at the top) in the BACK hand. Same face, same colors and same size as the attached sheet. He fights like a jiu-jitsu grappler from the countryside: grabs, pulls, takes down and twists; his kicks push people away; he hooks people with the crook. STAFF RULE: the crook is always in the same hand (the back hand, the arm drawn behind the torso) in all 8 frames unless the motion says otherwise; it never changes hands and never disappears.

Art style: hand-pixeled 1990s arcade fighting game sprite. Heroic anatomy: broad shoulders, thick strong torso, big hands and feet; he is the heavy grappler of the cast, stocky and solid but not fat. Shading in 2-3 large hard-edged flat planes per form, no gradients, no dithering, no noise, no texture. Shadows shift hue: skin shadows toward wine red, highlights toward warm cream; white clothes get cool lilac shadows; black clothes get blue-grey highlights. 1-pixel colored outline (dark wine on skin, dark blue-purple on clothes), never pure black, lighter on the lit side. Small hard 1-3 pixel highlights. Limited palette look.

Layout: one single row of 8 frames in 8 equal-width cells, wide image, side view, always facing right, same size and scale as the attached sheet, feet on the same ground line unless the move leaves the ground. The figure FILLS its cell (big and sharp, never a small figure). Each figure stays inside its own cell and never touches the next one. Flat solid magenta background (#FF00FF) everywhere, no shadow, no ground, no text, no numbers, no borders, no effects unless the motion asks for them.

Consecutive frames differ by a small amount, so the motion looks smooth in sequence.

Anatomy check for every frame: exactly two arms and two legs, both hands always visible, elbows and knees bend the natural way, no extra or missing limbs, the body never twists into an impossible pose.

Fighting-game timing (attacks): a clear anticipation pose before the hit, at most one stretched smear pose right before contact, the contact pose with the striking limb (or the staff) fully extended and drawn about 15% bigger, then a recovery that is longer on heavy moves; the brown belt ends and the hair fringe lag 1-2 frames behind the body and settle.

MOTION: grappler idle loop, the base reference for every other sheet: wide low stance, knees bent, both open hands forward ready to grab, the crook held low in the back hand, calm half-smile, slow heavy breathing. Frames 1 to 4 he sinks slightly, 5 to 8 he rises; frame 8 flows back into frame 1. Standing tall, the figure fills the cell height as described below.

SIZE: the image is 2172x724 pixels, 8 cells of 271 pixels each. Draw the figure BIG, standing, about 545 pixels from the soles to the top of the hair, soles at about y=655. Head, torso and limbs keep the same proportions in every frame. Every frame sits in its own column with at least 20 pixels of empty magenta between neighbours; when a pose is wide (kicks, lying down, rolls) bend the knees or lean so it fits in its cell without shrinking the body. Do NOT draw motion lines, speed lines, impact flashes, dust or any other effect: only the character and what he holds.
```

Shading in 2-3 large hard-edged flat planes per form, no gradients, no dithering, no noise, no texture. Layout: one single row of 8 frames in 8 equal-width cells, wide image, side view, always facing right, same size and scale as the attached sheet, feet on the same ground line unless the move leaves the ground. Consecutive frames differ by a small amount, so the motion looks smooth in sequence.

Fighting-game timing (attacks): a clear anticipation pose before the hit, at most one stretched smear pose right before contact, the contact pose with the striking limb (or the staff) fully extended and drawn about 15% bigger, then a recovery that is longer on heavy moves; the brown belt ends and the hair fringe lag 1-2 frames behind the body and settle.

The skull chest plate and the hooves must read clearly in the side view. No motorcycle items of any kind. No text, no logos. Same face, same colors and same size as the attached sheet. Shading in 2-3 large hard-edged flat planes per form, no gradients, no dithering, no noise, no texture. NEW OUTFIT: a CHEST PLATE MADE OF A GOAT SKULL, yellowed cracked bone, covering the chest, with the skull's eye sockets glowing green; SHOULDER GUARDS made of curved goat horns on both shoulders; BONE BRACERS on both forearms; two iron CHAINS crossed over the chest, fastened to the skull; bare muscular arms and bare belly sides; ripped BLACK LEATHER PANTS with green embers glowing inside the rips; GOAT HOOVES instead of feet, black and cracked, with green ember light in the cracks; a CAPE OF BLACK FUR with a tall thick fur collar around the neck, its hem in tatters. The skull chest plate and the hooves must read clearly in the side view. No motorcycle items of any kind. No text, no logos. Same face, same colors and same size as the attached sheet. He is the final boss version of the jiu-jitsu shepherd: slow, heavy and overwhelming, every move must look far too powerful; he grabs, lifts and slams. CROOK RULE: the bone crook is always in the same hand (the back hand, the arm drawn behind the torso) in all 8 frames unless the motion says otherwise; it never changes hands and never disappears. The goat-skull chest plate and the hooves stay visible in every frame; the fur cape follows the motion and never hides the chest plate. Do NOT draw the aura, the goat shadow or the giant green hands: they are separate effects.

Art style: hand-pixeled 1990s arcade fighting game sprite. Heroic anatomy: broad shoulders, thick strong torso, big hands and feet; he is the heavy grappler of the cast, stocky and solid but not fat. Shading in 2-3 large hard-edged flat planes per form, no gradients, no dithering, no noise, no texture. Small hard 1-3 pixel highlights. Limited palette look.

Layout: one single row of 8 frames in 8 equal-width cells, wide image, side view, always facing right, same size and scale as the attached sheet, feet on the same ground line unless the move leaves the ground. The figure FILLS its cell (big and sharp, never a small figure). Each figure stays inside its own cell and never touches the next one. Flat solid magenta background (#FF00FF) everywhere, no shadow, no ground, no text, no numbers, no borders, no effects unless the motion asks for them.

Consecutive frames differ by a small amount, so the motion looks smooth in sequence.

Fighting-game timing (attacks): a clear anticipation pose before the hit, at most one stretched smear pose right before contact, the contact pose with the striking limb (or the staff) fully extended and drawn about 15% bigger, then a recovery that is longer on heavy moves; the brown belt ends and the hair fringe lag 1-2 frames behind the body and settle.

Shadows shift hue: skin shadows toward wine red, highlights toward warm cream; white clothes get cool lilac shadows; black clothes get blue-grey highlights. 1-pixel colored outline (dark wine on skin, dark blue-purple on clothes), never pure black, lighter on the lit side. Small hard 1-3 pixel highlights. Limited palette look.

Layout: one single row of 8 frames in 8 equal-width cells, wide image, side view, always facing right, same size and scale as the attached sheet, feet on the same ground line unless the move leaves the ground. The figure FILLS its cell (big and sharp, never a small figure). Each figure stays inside its own cell and never touches the next one. Flat solid magenta background (#FF00FF) everywhere, no shadow, no ground, no text, no numbers, no borders, no effects unless the motion asks for them.

Consecutive frames differ by a small amount, so the motion looks smooth in sequence.

Anatomy check for every frame: exactly two arms and two legs, both hands always visible, elbows and knees bend the natural way, no extra or missing limbs, the body never twists into an impossible pose.

Fighting-game timing (attacks): a clear anticipation pose before the hit, at most one stretched smear pose right before contact, the contact pose with the striking limb (or the staff) fully extended and drawn about 15% bigger, then a recovery that is longer on heavy moves; the brown belt ends and the hair fringe lag 1-2 frames behind the body and settle.

MOTION: being grabbed and thrown by an unseen opponent, no other character drawn: 1 standing hit, torso doubled forward, 2 lifted with the hooves off the ground, arms flailing, 3 tilted backward 45 degrees in the air, 4 upside down, horns toward the ground, 5 horizontal face up in the air, 6 horizontal face down in the air, 7 curled and spinning, knees to chest, 8 stretched out face down just above the ground; the cape and the belt flames follow the motion and the crook stays in the hand.

SIZE: the image is 2172x724 pixels, 8 cells of 271 pixels each. Draw the figure BIG, at exactly the same pixel size as the attached sheet: standing, about 545 pixels from the soles to the top of the hair (the horns go above that), soles at about y=655. Head, torso and limbs keep the same proportions in every frame. Every frame sits in its own column with at least 20 pixels of empty magenta between neighbours; when a pose is wide (kicks, lying down, rolls) bend the knees or lean so it fits in its cell without shrinking the body. Do NOT draw motion lines, speed lines, impact flashes, dust or any other effect: only the character and what he holds.
```
